# Research: subset-b-000589

Grouped research for ceph-client Linux MFD Devicetree binding YAML schemas. Each section preserves the source path and is wrapped for deterministic reconciliation into source-tree-aligned per-file reports.

<!-- BEGIN_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,bcm63268-gpio-sysctl.yaml -->
# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,bcm63268-gpio-sysctl.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,bcm63268-gpio-sysctl.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Broadcom BCM63268 GPIO System Controller`, a GPIO, pinctrl, or system-control MFD binding. Broadcom BCM63268 SoC GPIO system controller which provides a register map for controlling the GPIO and pins of the SoC. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence and covers 3 compatible tokens: `brcm,bcm63268-gpio-sysctl`, `syscon`, `simple-mfd`. Top-level properties are `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `ranges`, `reg`. Top-level required properties are `#address-cells`, `compatible`, `ranges`, `reg`, `#size-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `ranges`, `reg`. The node acts as a MMIO/syscon parent node with address-translated child functions. Important property roles: `#address-cells` defines child-node address layout; `#size-cells` defines child-node address layout; `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `ranges` maps child register offsets below the MFD/syscon parent; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range. Named child-function entries are no named child-function nodes detected; patterned child schemas are `^gpio@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^pinctrl@[0-9a-f]+$`. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Álvaro Fernández Rojas <noltari@gmail.com>, Jonas Gorski <jonas.gorski@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/gpio/brcm,bcm63xx-gpio.yaml`, `/schemas/pinctrl/brcm,bcm63268-pinctrl.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_imx.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_mtk.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/pata_ixp4xx_cf.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/sata_gemini.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/auxdisplay/img-ascii-lcd.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/ath3k.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/btusb.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/arm-integrator-lm.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/imx-weim.c`, and 3 more. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-r40.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-v3s.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sunxi-h3-h5.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amazon/alpine.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8b.dtsi`, and 3 more. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,bcm63268-gpio-sysctl.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,bcm63268-gpio-sysctl.yaml -->

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,bcm6362-gpio-sysctl.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,bcm6368-gpio-sysctl.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,bcm6368-gpio-sysctl.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Broadcom BCM6368 GPIO System Controller`, a GPIO, pinctrl, or system-control MFD binding. Broadcom BCM6368 SoC GPIO system controller which provides a register map for controlling the GPIO and pins of the SoC. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence and covers 3 compatible tokens: `brcm,bcm6368-gpio-sysctl`, `syscon`, `simple-mfd`. Top-level properties are `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `ranges`, `reg`. Top-level required properties are `#address-cells`, `compatible`, `ranges`, `reg`, `#size-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `ranges`, `reg`. The node acts as a MMIO/syscon parent node with address-translated child functions. Important property roles: `#address-cells` defines child-node address layout; `#size-cells` defines child-node address layout; `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `ranges` maps child register offsets below the MFD/syscon parent; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range. Named child-function entries are no named child-function nodes detected; patterned child schemas are `^gpio@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^pinctrl@[0-9a-f]+$`. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Álvaro Fernández Rojas <noltari@gmail.com>, Jonas Gorski <jonas.gorski@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/gpio/brcm,bcm63xx-gpio.yaml`, `/schemas/pinctrl/brcm,bcm6368-pinctrl.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. **Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,twd.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Broadcom's Timer-Watchdog (aka TWD)`, a timer/watchdog MFD binding. Broadcom has a Timer-Watchdog block used in multiple SoCs (e.g., BCM4908, BCM63xx, BCM7038). There are few variants available (they differ slightly in registers layout). Named child-function entries are no named child-function nodes detected; patterned child schemas are `^timer@[a-f0-9]+$`, `^watchdog@[a-f0-9]+$`. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Rafał Miłecki <rafal@milecki.pl>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/timer/brcm,bcmbca-timer.yaml`, `/schemas/watchdog/brcm,bcm7038-wdt.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_imx.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_mtk.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/pata_ixp4xx_cf.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/sata_gemini.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/auxdisplay/img-ascii-lcd.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/ath3k.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/btusb.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/arm-integrator-lm.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/imx-weim.c`, and 3 more. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-r40.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-v3s.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sunxi-h3-h5.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amazon/alpine.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8b.dtsi`, and 3 more. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/brcm,twd.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/canaan,k210-sysctl.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Canaan Kendryte K210 System Controller`, a system-controller/syscon MFD binding. Canaan Inc. Kendryte K210 SoC system controller which provides a register map for controlling the clocks, reset signals and pin power domains of the SoC. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

Named child-function entries are `clock-controller`, `clocks`, `reset-controller`, `syscon-reboot`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/cirrus,lochnagar.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/cirrus,lochnagar.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Cirrus Logic Lochnagar Audio Development Board`, a power-management MFD binding. Lochnagar is an evaluation and development board for Cirrus Logic Smart CODEC and Amp devices. It allows the connection of most Cirrus Logic devices on mini-cards, as well as allowing connection of various application processor systems to provide a full evaluation platform. Audio system topology, clocking and power can all be controlled through the Lochnagar, allowing the device under test to be used in a variety of possible use cases. Also see these documents for generic binding information: [1] GPIO : ../gpio/gpio.txt And these for relevant defines: [2] include/dt-bindings/pinctrl/lochnagar.h [3] include/dt-bindings/clock/lochnagar.h And these documents for the required sub-node binding details: [4] Clock: ../clock/cirrus,lochnagar.yaml [5] Pinc... Named child-function entries are `lochnagar-clk`, `lochnagar-hwmon`, `pinctrl`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`, `minimum=1`, `maximum=4`.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/delta,tn48m-cpld.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/delta,tn48m-cpld.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Delta Networks TN48M CPLD controller`, a multi-function device binding. Lattice CPLD onboard the TN48M switches is used for system management. It provides information about the hardware model, revision, PSU status etc. It is also being used as a GPIO expander and reset controller for the switch MAC-s and other peripherals. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `delta,tn48m-cpld`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `reg`. The node acts as a MMIO/syscon parent node with address-translated child functions. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `#address-cells` defines child-node address layout; `#size-cells` defines child-node address layout. Named child-function entries are no named child-function nodes detected; patterned child schemas are `^gpio(@[0-9a-f]+)?$`, `^reset-controller?$`. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Robert Marko <robert.marko@sartura.hr>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/gpio/delta,tn48m-gpio.yaml`, `/schemas/reset/delta,tn48m-reset.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/simple-mfd-i2c.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/delta,tn48m-cpld.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/delta,tn48m-cpld.yaml -->

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/dlg,da9063.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Dialog DA906{3L,3,2,1} Power Management Integrated Circuit (PMIC)`, a power-management MFD binding. For device-tree bindings of other sub-modules refer to the binding documents under the respective sub-system directories. Using regulator-{uv,ov}-{warn,error,protection}-microvolt requires special handling: First, when GP_FB2 is used, it must be ensured that there is no moment where all voltage monitors are disabled. Next, as da9063 only supports UV *and* OV monitoring, both must be set to the same severity and value (0: disable, 1: enable). Product information for the DA906{3L,3,2,1} devices can be found here: - https://www.dialog-semiconductor.com/products/da9063l - https://www.dialog-semiconductor.com/products/da9063 - https://www.dialog-semiconductor.com/products/da9062 - https://www.dialog-semiconductor.com/products/da9061 It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. The node acts as a I2C-addressed companion device or PMIC node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `interrupt-controller` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `#interrupt-cells` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `gpio-controller` exposes or names GPIO controller resources; `#gpio-cells` exposes or names GPIO controller resources; `onkey` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `regulators` contains regulator child nodes; `rtc` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `thermal` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `watchdog` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice. Named child-function entries are `gpio`, `onkey`, `regulators`, `rtc`, `thermal`, `watchdog`; patterned child schemas are `^(.+-hog(-[0-9]+)?)$`. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. **State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. **Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. This schema has 3 top-level properties and 3 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/ene-kb930.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/fsl,imx25-tsadc.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/fsl,imx25-tsadc.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Freescale MX25 ADC/TSC MultiFunction Device (MFD)`, a ADC, touchscreen, or sensor MFD binding. This device combines two general purpose conversion queues one used for general ADC and the other used for touchscreens. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `fsl,imx25-tsadc`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `interrupt-controller`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `ranges`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#address-cells`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#size-cells`, `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`. The node acts as a MMIO/syscon parent node with address-translated child functions. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `clocks` integrates clock provider or consumer wiring; `clock-names` integrates clock provider or consumer wiring; `interrupt-controller` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `#interrupt-cells` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `#address-cells` defines child-node address layout; `#size-cells` defines child-node address layout; `ranges` maps child register offsets below the MFD/syscon parent. Named child-function entries are `clocks`; patterned child schemas are `^adc@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^touchscreen@[0-9a-f]+$`. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Frank Li <Frank.Li@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/iio/adc/fsl,imx25-gcq.yaml`, `/schemas/input/touchscreen/fsl,imx25-tcq.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/fsl-imx25-tsadc.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx25.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 10 top-level properties and 10 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/fsl,imx25-tsadc.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/iei,wt61p803-puzzle.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/iei,wt61p803-puzzle.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `IEI WT61P803 PUZZLE MCU from IEI Integration Corp.`, a embedded-controller or board MCU MFD binding. IEI WT61P803 PUZZLE MCU is embedded in some IEI Puzzle series boards. It's used for controlling system power states, fans, LEDs and temperature sensors. For Device Tree bindings of other sub-modules (HWMON, LEDs) refer to the binding documents under the respective subsystem directories. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `iei,wt61p803-puzzle`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `current-speed`, `enable-beep`, `hwmon`, `leds`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `current-speed`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `compatible`, `current-speed`. The node acts as a UART/serial-attached controller node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `hwmon` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `leds` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice. Named child-function entries are `hwmon`, `leds`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Luka Kovacic <luka.kovacic@sartura.hr>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/hwmon/iei,wt61p803-puzzle-hwmon.yaml`, `/schemas/leds/iei,wt61p803-puzzle-leds.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: no direct in-tree driver match found by compatible scan. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/marvell/armada-8040-puzzle-m801.dts`. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/iei,wt61p803-puzzle.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/iei,wt61p803-puzzle.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max14577.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max14577.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Maxim MAX14577/MAX77836 MicroUSB and Companion Power Management IC`, a power-management MFD binding. This is a part of device tree bindings for Maxim MAX14577/MAX77836 MicroUSB Integrated Circuit (MUIC). The Maxim MAX14577 is a MicroUSB and Companion Power Management IC which includes voltage safeout regulators, charger and MicroUSB management IC. The Maxim MAX77836 is a MicroUSB and Companion Power Management IC which includes voltage safeout and LDO regulators, charger, fuel-gauge and MicroUSB management IC. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses `enum` list and covers 2 compatible tokens: `maxim,max14577`, `maxim,max77836`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `wakeup-source`, `charger`, `extcon`, `regulators`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `charger`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `charger`, `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C-addressed companion device or PMIC node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `charger` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `extcon` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `regulators` contains regulator child nodes. Named child-function entries are `charger`, `extcon`, `regulators`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Krzysztof Kozlowski <krzk@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/power/supply/maxim,max14577.yaml`, `/schemas/regulator/maxim,max14577.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/extcon/extcon-max14577.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/max14577.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/max14577_charger.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/max17040_battery.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos3250-monk.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos3250-rinato.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/s5pv210-fascinate4g.dts`. The file includes 2 example blocks.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 7 top-level properties and 8 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max14577.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max14577.yaml -->

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77686.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Maxim MAX77686 Power Management IC`, a power-management MFD binding. This is a part of device tree bindings for Maxim MAX77686 Power Management Integrated Circuit (PMIC). The Maxim MAX77686 is a Power Management IC which includes voltage and current regulators, RTC and clock outputs. The MAX77686 provides three 32.768khz clock outputs that can be controlled (gated/ungated) over I2C. The clock IDs are defined as preprocessor macros in dt-bindings/clock/maxim,max77686.h. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `#clock-cells`, `reg`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#clock-cells`, `compatible`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C-addressed companion device or PMIC node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `#clock-cells` integrates clock provider or consumer wiring; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `voltage-regulators` contains regulator child nodes. Named child-function entries are `voltage-regulators`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. **State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Chanwoo Choi <cw00.choi@samsung.com>, Krzysztof Kozlowski <krzk@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/regulator/maxim,max77686.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/clk/clk-max77686.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/max77686.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos4412-midas.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos4412-odroid-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos4412-p4note.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos5250-manta.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos5250-smdk5250.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos5250-snow-common.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 6 top-level properties and 6 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77686.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77686.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77693.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77693.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Maxim MAX77693 MicroUSB and Companion Power Management IC`, a power-management MFD binding. This is a part of device tree bindings for Maxim MAX77693 MicroUSB Integrated Circuit (MUIC). The Maxim MAX77693 is a MicroUSB and Companion Power Management IC which includes voltage current regulators, charger, LED/flash, haptic motor driver and MicroUSB management IC. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `maxim,max77693`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `charger`, `led`, `max77693-muic`, `muic`, `motor-driver`, `regulators`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `compatible`, `haptic-supply`, `interrupts`, `pwms`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C-addressed companion device or PMIC node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `charger` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `muic` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `regulators` contains regulator child nodes. Named child-function entries are `charger`, `led`, `max77693-muic`, `motor-driver`, `muic`, `regulators`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Chanwoo Choi <cw00.choi@samsung.com>, Krzysztof Kozlowski <krzk@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/connector/usb-connector.yaml#`, `/schemas/leds/maxim,max77693.yaml`, `/schemas/power/supply/maxim,max77693.yaml`, `/schemas/regulator/maxim,max77693.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/extcon/extcon-max77693.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/input/misc/max77693-haptic.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/leds/flash/leds-max77693.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/max77693.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos4212-tab3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos4412-midas.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 9 top-level properties and 12 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77693.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77802.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77802.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Maxim MAX77802 Power Management IC`, a power-management MFD binding. This is a part of device tree bindings for Maxim MAX77802 Power Management Integrated Circuit (PMIC). The Maxim MAX77802 is a Power Management IC which includes voltage and current regulators (10 high efficiency Buck regulators and 32 Low-DropOut (LDO)), RTC and clock outputs. The MAX77802 provides two 32.768khz clock outputs that can be controlled (gated/ungated) over I2C. The clock IDs are defined as preprocessor macros in dt-bindings/clock/maxim,max77802.h. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `maxim,max77802`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `#clock-cells`, `interrupts`, `reg`, `regulators`, `inb1-supply`, `inb2-supply`, `inb3-supply`, `inb4-supply`, `inb5-supply`, `inb6-supply`, `inb7-supply`, `inb8-supply`, `inb9-supply`, `inb10-supply`, `inl1-supply`, `inl2-supply`, `inl3-supply`, `inl4-supply`, `inl5-supply`, `inl6-supply`, `inl7-supply`, `inl9-supply`, `inl10-supply`, `wakeup-source`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `#clock-cells`, `reg`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#clock-cells`, `compatible`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C-addressed companion device or PMIC node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `#clock-cells` integrates clock provider or consumer wiring; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `regulators` contains regulator child nodes; `inb1-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `inb2-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `inb3-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `inb4-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `inb5-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `inb6-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `inb7-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `inb8-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `inb9-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; and 10 more resource/property roles. Named child-function entries are `regulators`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Javier Martinez Canillas <javier@dowhile0.org>, Krzysztof Kozlowski <krzk@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/regulator/maxim,max77802.yaml`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/clk/clk-max77686.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/max77686.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/regulator/max77802-regulator.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos5410-odroidxu.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos5420-peach-pit.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/samsung/exynos5800-peach-pi.dts`. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 25 top-level properties and 25 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77802.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/maxim,max77802.yaml -->

The Maxim MAX8998 is a Power Management IC which includes voltage/current regulators, real time clock, battery charging controller and several other sub-blocks. It is interfaced using an I2C interface. Each sub-block is addressed by the host system using different i2c slave address. **Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/mediatek,mt6357.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MediaTek MT6357 PMIC`, a power-management MFD binding. MT6357 is a power management system chip containing 5 buck converters and 29 LDOs. Supported features are audio codec, USB battery charging, fuel gauge, RTC This is a multifunction device with the following sub modules: - Regulator - RTC - Keys It is interfaced to host controller using SPI interface by a proprietary hardware called PMIC wrapper or pwrap. This MFD is a child device of pwrap. See the following for pwrap node definitions: Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,pwrap.yaml It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `interrupt-controller` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `#interrupt-cells` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `regulators` contains regulator child nodes; `rtc` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `keys` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice. Named child-function entries are `keys`, `regulators`, `rtc`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/mediatek,mt6360.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/mediatek,mt6360.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MT6360 PMIC from MediaTek Integrated`, a power-management MFD binding. MT6360 is a PMIC device with the following sub modules. It is interfaced to host controller using I2C interface. This document describes the binding for PMIC device and its sub module. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `mediatek,mt6360`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `wakeup-source`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-names`, `interrupt-controller`, `#interrupt-cells`, `regulators`, `charger`, `tcpc`, `led-controller`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `interrupt-controller`, `#interrupt-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#interrupt-cells`, `compatible`, `interrupt-controller`, `interrupts`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C-addressed companion device or PMIC node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `interrupt-names` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `interrupt-controller` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `#interrupt-cells` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `regulators` contains regulator child nodes; `charger` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `tcpc` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice; `led-controller` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice. Named child-function entries are `charger`, `led-controller`, `regulators`, `tcpc`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.
