At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: AngeloGioacchino Del Regno <angelogioacchino.delregno@collabora.com>, Matthias Brugger <matthias.bgg@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/power/mediatek,power-controller.yaml#`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_imx.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_mtk.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/pata_ixp4xx_cf.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/sata_gemini.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/auxdisplay/img-ascii-lcd.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/ath3k.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/btusb.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/arm-integrator-lm.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/imx-weim.c`, and 3 more. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-r40.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-v3s.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sunxi-h3-h5.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amazon/alpine.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8b.dtsi`, and 3 more. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 3 top-level properties and 3 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/mediatek,mt8195-scpsys.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.

MP2629 is a PMIC providing battery charging and power supply for smartphones, wireless camera and portable devices. Chip is controlled over I2C. Named child-function entries are no named child-function nodes detected; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

The Ocelot ethernet switch family contains chips that have an internal CPU (VSC7513, VSC7514) and chips that don't (VSC7511, VSC7512). # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/nxp,bbnsm.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/nxp,bbnsm.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `NXP Battery-Backed Non-Secure Module`, a multi-function device binding. NXP BBNSM serves as non-volatile logic and storage for the system. it Intergrates RTC & ON/OFF control. The RTC can retain its state and continues counting even when the main chip is power down. A time alarm is generated once the most significant 32 bits of the real-time counter match the value in the Time Alarm register. The ON/OFF logic inside the BBNSM allows for connecting directly to a PMIC or other voltage regulator device. both smart PMIC mode and Dumb PMIC mode supported. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence and covers 3 compatible tokens: `nxp,imx93-bbnsm`, `syscon`, `simple-mfd`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `rtc`, `pwrkey`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `rtc`, `pwrkey`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `compatible`, `interrupts`, `pwrkey`, `reg`, `rtc`. The node acts as a bus or MMIO node selected by parent address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `rtc` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice. Named child-function entries are `pwrkey`, `rtc`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Jacky Bai <ping.bai@nxp.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/input/input.yaml#`, `/schemas/rtc/rtc.yaml#`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_imx.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_mtk.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/pata_ixp4xx_cf.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/sata_gemini.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/auxdisplay/img-ascii-lcd.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/ath3k.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/btusb.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/arm-integrator-lm.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/imx-weim.c`, and 3 more. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-r40.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-v3s.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sunxi-h3-h5.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amazon/alpine.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8b.dtsi`, and 3 more. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 4 top-level properties and 7 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/nxp,bbnsm.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/qcom,pm8008.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/qcom,pm8008.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm Technologies, Inc. PM8008 PMIC`, a power-management MFD binding. Qualcomm Technologies, Inc. PM8008 is a dedicated camera PMIC that integrates all the necessary power management, housekeeping, and interface support functions into a single IC. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `qcom,pm8008`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `reset-gpios`, `vdd-l1-l2-supply`, `vdd-l3-l4-supply`, `vdd-l5-supply`, `vdd-l6-supply`, `vdd-l7-supply`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`, `gpio-ranges`, `interrupt-controller`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#thermal-sensor-cells`, `pinctrl`, `regulators`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `vdd-l1-l2-supply`, `vdd-l3-l4-supply`, `vdd-l5-supply`, `vdd-l6-supply`, `vdd-l7-supply`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`, `gpio-ranges`, `interrupt-controller`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#thermal-sensor-cells`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `#gpio-cells`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#thermal-sensor-cells`, `compatible`, `function`, `gpio-controller`, `gpio-ranges`, `interrupt-controller`, `interrupts`, `pins`, `reg`, `vdd-l1-l2-supply`, `vdd-l3-l4-supply`, `vdd-l5-supply`, `vdd-l6-supply`, `vdd-l7-supply`. The node acts as a I2C-addressed companion device or PMIC node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `reset-gpios` describes control/status GPIO wiring; `vdd-l1-l2-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `vdd-l3-l4-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `vdd-l5-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `vdd-l6-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `vdd-l7-supply` links supply rails to the regulator framework; `gpio-controller` exposes or names GPIO controller resources; `#gpio-cells` exposes or names GPIO controller resources; `gpio-ranges` exposes or names GPIO controller resources; `interrupt-controller` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `#interrupt-cells` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; and 2 more resource/property roles. Named child-function entries are `pinctrl`, `regulators`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Guru Das Srinagesh <quic_gurus@quicinc.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/pinctrl/pincfg-node.yaml`, `/schemas/pinctrl/pinmux-node.yaml`, `/schemas/regulator/regulator.yaml#`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/qcom-pm8008.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/milos-fairphone-fp6.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/qcm6490-fairphone-fp5.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/qrb2210-rb1.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sc8280xp-lenovo-thinkpad-x13s.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sm7225-fairphone-fp4.dts`. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 17 top-level properties and 19 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/qcom,pm8008.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/qcom,pm8008.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/qcom,spmi-pmic.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/qcom,spmi-pmic.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Qualcomm SPMI PMICs multi-function device`, a power-management MFD binding. Some Qualcomm PMICs used with the Snapdragon series SoCs are interfaced to the chip via the SPMI (System Power Management Interface) bus. Support for multiple independent functions are implemented by splitting the 16-bit SPMI peripheral address space into 256 smaller fixed-size regions, 256 bytes each. A function can consume one or more of these fixed-size register regions. The Qualcomm SPMI series includes the PM8941, PM8841, PMA8084, PM8998 and other PMICs. These PMICs use a "QPNP" scheme through SPMI interface. QPNP is effectively a partitioning scheme for dividing the SPMI extended register space up into logical pieces, and set of fixed register locations/definitions within these regions, with some of these regions specifically used for interr... It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence and covers 75 compatible tokens: `qcom,pm2250`, `qcom,pm6125`, `qcom,pm6150`, `qcom,pm6150l`, `qcom,pm6350`, `qcom,pm660`, `qcom,pm660l`, `qcom,pm7250b`, `qcom,pm7550ba`, `qcom,pm7325`, `qcom,pm7550`, `qcom,pm8004`, `qcom,pm8005`, `qcom,pm8009`, `qcom,pm8010`, `qcom,pm8019`, `qcom,pm8028`, `qcom,pm8110`, `qcom,pm8150`, `qcom,pm8150b`, `qcom,pm8150c`, `qcom,pm8150l`, and 53 more. Top-level properties are `$nodename`, `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `labibb`, `regulators`, `pwm`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `compatible`, `reg`. The node acts as a SPMI peripheral node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `#address-cells` defines child-node address layout; `#size-cells` defines child-node address layout; `regulators` contains regulator child nodes; `pwm` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice. Named child-function entries are `labibb`, `pwm`, `regulators`; patterned child schemas are `(.*)?(wled|leds)@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^adc-tm@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^adc@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^audio-codec@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^battery@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^charger@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^led-controller@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^mpps@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^nvram@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^pbs@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^rtc@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^temp-alarm@[0-9a-f]+$`, and 7 more. Numeric and item constraints include `minItems=1`, `maxItems=2`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Stephen Boyd <sboyd@kernel.org>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/extcon/qcom,pm8941-misc.yaml#`, `/schemas/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-adc5-gen3.yaml#`, `/schemas/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-iadc.yaml#`, `/schemas/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-rradc.yaml#`, `/schemas/iio/adc/qcom,spmi-vadc.yaml#`, `/schemas/input/qcom,pm8xxx-vib.yaml#`, `/schemas/leds/backlight/qcom-wled.yaml#`, `/schemas/leds/leds-qcom-lpg.yaml#`, `/schemas/leds/qcom,spmi-flash-led.yaml#`, `/schemas/nvmem/qcom,spmi-sdam.yaml#`, `/schemas/phy/qcom,snps-eusb2-repeater.yaml#`, `/schemas/pinctrl/qcom,pmic-gpio.yaml#`, `/schemas/pinctrl/qcom,pmic-mpp.yaml#`, `/schemas/power/reset/qcom,pon.yaml#`, `/schemas/power/supply/qcom,pm8916-bms-vm.yaml#`, `/schemas/power/supply/qcom,pm8916-lbc.yaml#`, `/schemas/power/supply/qcom,pm8941-charger.yaml#`, `/schemas/power/supply/qcom,pm8941-coincell.yaml#`, and 11 more. Top-level composition and conditional keys are `allOf`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/richtek,rt4831.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/richtek,rt4831.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Richtek RT4831 DSV and Backlight Integrated IC`, a power-management MFD binding. RT4831 is a multifunctional device that can provide power to the LCD display and LCD backlight. For Display Bias Voltage DSVP and DSVN, the output range is about 4V to 6.5V. It's sufficient to meet the current LCD power requirement. For the LCD backlight, it can provide four channel WLED driving capability. Each channel driving current is up to 30mA Datasheet is available at https://www.richtek.com/assets/product_file/RT4831A/DS4831A-05.pdf It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

Named child-function entries are `backlight`, `regulators`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. **State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/richtek,rt4831.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/richtek,rt5033.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/richtek,rt5033.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Richtek RT5033 Power Management Integrated Circuit`, a power-management MFD binding. RT5033 is a multifunction device which includes battery charger, fuel gauge, flash LED current source, LDO and synchronous Buck converter for portable applications. It is interfaced to host controller using I2C interface. The battery fuel gauge uses a separate I2C bus. It is declarative ABI documentation and dt-schema validation input, not executable kernel code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe the MFD parent and any child functions so the kernel can bind the parent driver, instantiate child devices, and hand common resources to subsystem drivers.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, child schemas, references, examples, and compatible-specific composition. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `richtek,rt5033`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `regulators`, `charger`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`; required properties discovered across nested schemas include `compatible`, `interrupts`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C-addressed companion device or PMIC node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C/SPI/SPMI address or MMIO range; `interrupts` describes IRQ routing or IRQ-controller behavior; `regulators` contains regulator child nodes; `charger` is a child-function schema entry for a subdevice. Named child-function entries are `charger`, `regulators`; patterned child schemas are none. Numeric and item constraints include `maxItems=1`.

**Control flow:** Control flow is supplied by Devicetree tooling and by the Linux MFD/subsystem core. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves `$ref` targets, applies property constraints, validates `required` sets and examples, evaluates `allOf`/`oneOf`/conditional branches, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At boot the compiled DTB node is matched through an OF compatible table; the parent MFD, syscon, PMIC, SPMI, I2C, SPI, or platform driver then reads `reg`, clocks, interrupts, resets, GPIOs, supplies, and child nodes before registering subdevices such as regulators, RTCs, GPIO/pinctrl blocks, chargers, watchdogs, LEDs, ADCs, PHYs, thermal sensors, or power/reset controllers.

**State and persistence behavior:** The YAML owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: compatible tokens, child-node names, required supply/clock/IRQ/reset resources, phandle cell counts, register ranges, and fallback ordering become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Real state such as regulator voltage, watchdog countdown, RTC time, GPIO direction, charger status, reset line assertion, NVMEM contents, or interrupt masks is maintained by the hardware and kernel drivers that consume nodes validated by this schema.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Jakob Hauser <jahau@rocketmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are `/schemas/power/supply/richtek,rt5033-charger.yaml#`, `/schemas/regulator/regulator.yaml#`. Top-level composition and conditional keys are none. Integration points include OF matching through `compatible`, bus enumeration through parent addressing, `mfd-core` child creation where a parent driver is present, syscon/regmap access for shared registers, and subsystem handoff to regulator, GPIO, pinctrl, reset, clock, RTC, input, power-supply, thermal, watchdog, LED, IIO, USB/Type-C, PHY, NVMEM, network, or sound bindings referenced here. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/leds/flash/leds-sgm3140.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mfd/rt5033.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/rt5033_battery.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/power/supply/rt5033_charger.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8916-samsung-a2015-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8916-samsung-fortuna-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8916-samsung-gprimeltecan.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8916-samsung-serranove.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8939-samsung-a7.dts`. The file includes 1 example block.

**Risks:** Main risks are Devicetree ABI drift and parent/child contract skew. Tightening required properties can reject old board files; relaxing constraints can hide misspelled resources until probe fails. Compatible fallback ordering must match driver tables and documented hardware lineage. Child-function names and `patternProperties` must stay aligned with the subdriver bindings they reference, especially for regulators, GPIO/pinctrl, RTC, charger, LED, Type-C, watchdog, power/reset, and syscon children. Register ranges, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, and phandle cell counts are high-risk because they affect child resource decoding. Closure profile: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This schema has 5 top-level properties and 5 distinct nested property names, so broad edits should be validated against every compatible variant and every in-tree DTS user rather than only the example node.

**Test signals:** Primary validation is `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/mfd/richtek,rt5033.yaml` plus `make dtbs_check` for DTS files that instantiate each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required `reg`, IRQ, supply, clock, reset, GPIO, address-cell, or child-function properties; misspell child node names; use bad compatible fallback order; and exercise every conditional branch. Runtime signals are successful parent probe, expected child devices under sysfs/debugfs, working regulator/RTC/GPIO/pinctrl/reset/clock/charger/watchdog/LED/thermal/USB or other referenced subdrivers, and no new dtbs_check warnings for existing board examples.

Ricoh RN5T567/RN5T618/RC5T619 is a power management IC family which integrates 3 to 5 step-down DCDC converters, 7 to 10 low-dropout regulators, GPIOs, and a watchdog timer.
